# REGRESSION_LEDGER.md

Per LOOP_ENGINEERING.md: every fix ships a regression test plus one line here
(`date | symptom | root cause | rule broken | locking test`). Append-only. Earlier
phase regressions live as named tests (tests/unit/critic*.test.ts) + docs/DECISIONS.md;
this ledger starts at the 2026-06-21 hardening session.

| date | symptom | root cause | rule broken | locking test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-03 | Owner: "fixed vs discretionary is not category based — not all hair and beauty is fixed; when I switch one transaction in this category to discretionary, they all do" | The #396 restoration made the verdict a per-CATEGORY designation (CategoryFixedOverride), so one row’s flip rewrote every transaction filed under that category | The class is a property of the TRANSACTION: the reader’s per-row verdict (`Transaction.spendClassOverride`) wins; the app guesses per row (recurring-bill merchant → fixed — the owner’s seed rule — else the category’s taxonomy flag); a dial choice matching the guess stores NULL so the guess stays the default | `test_regression__flipping_one_transaction_leaves_category_siblings_alone` + per-row `test_regression__user_override_moves_dining_into_fixed_median` + the recurring-guess cases in `tests/unit/spend-class.test.ts`; `tests/unit/transaction-spend-class-actions.test.ts` (real action: one-row flip, matching-guess-NULL, recurring-guess agreement, demo fence) |
| 2026-08-03 | The Fixed/Discretionary dial vanished from the register and transaction detail while the owner still wanted it — commit ad23e40 (#395) removed the dial, the `CategoryFixedOverride` table, and `setCategoryFixed` citing an owner directive ("deterministic and algorithmic, never typed in") the owner says he never gave | An "owner directive" was recorded in DECISIONS #395 and executed without tracing to words the owner actually said; the reversal restores the pre-#395 implementation from `ad23e40^` | No-fabrication prime directive — a claimed directive must trace to the owner; a change built on one that does not is reversed, not defended. The deterministic classifier stays the DEFAULT (matching choices delete the override row) | Restored locks: `test_regression__user_override_moves_dining_into_fixed_median` + `test_regression__custom_nondiscretionary_category_counts_as_fixed` in `tests/unit/spend-class.test.ts`; `test_regression__set_category_fixed_refuses_shared_demo` in `tests/unit/category-fixed-actions.test.ts`. | 2026-06-21 | `rateLimitDurable` let a CONCURRENT burst bypass the limit entirely (50/50 at limit 8) — the brute-force/export throttle did nothing under the conditions it exists for | the reset branch did `upsert{count:1}` + `return true` unconditionally, never reading a post-write count (TOCTOU) | "a security control must hold under its own threat model (concurrency)" | `tests/unit/rate-limit-durable.test.ts` (12-call `Promise.all` burst → exactly 4 allowed) |
| 2026-06-21 | `RateLimit` table grew unboundedly — one permanent row per attacker-guessed `signin:<email>`, no prune, no index (CWE-770, unauthenticated) | no reclamation path + attacker-controlled key space | "bound any attacker-reachable allocation" | `tests/unit/rate-limit-durable.test.ts` (`pruneExpiredRateLimits` deletes expired, keeps live) + `@@index([resetAt])` |
| 2026-06-21 | Two concurrent `splitTransaction` calls could double-split a row (children created twice → doubled in every aggregate) | `isSplitParent` was read BEFORE the transaction; both racers passed the pre-read | "guard data-integrity invariants inside the atomic boundary, not before it" | `tests/unit/split-race.test.ts` (parallel splits → exactly one set of children; verified to fail on pre-fix code) |

| 2026-06-21 | Email-keyed sign-in throttle let an attacker who knows a victim's address lock that account out (targeted-account DoS) | the only throttle dimension was `signin:<email>` checked BEFORE auth, so it blocked the victim's correct password too | "a throttle must not penalize the legitimate (correct-credential) user" | `tests/unit/rate-limit-durable.test.ts` (per-IP cap before auth + per-account-FAIL after; a correct password succeeds even after the fail budget is spent) |

| 2026-06-27 | e2e `transactions.spec.ts:145` (inline recat) flaked intermittently off OneDrive — after recat, the row sometimes still showed 'Dining Out' within the 5s window | the positive assert ran on the WHOLE ROW (`toContainText('Groceries')`) and matched the in-flight 'File as Groceries?' confirm prompt → passed before persistence, so the negative `not.toContainText('Dining Out')` raced `router.refresh()` on its default 5s budget (asymmetric vs the positive's 20s) | "assert on the element the test actually means (the chip), not text that includes transient sibling UI; give both sides the same generous budget" | tests/e2e/transactions.spec.ts:145 now asserts on `category-chip` (sibling-prompt-immune) with 20s on both assertions — app behavior verified correct (commit awaits recategorize then router.refresh); 4/4 full-suite runs green post-fix (DECISIONS #121) |
| 2026-06-28 | `parseTargetAmount` (new, #126) truncated any ungrouped 4+ digit `$` amount to its first 3 digits — "save $20000 by 2028" parsed as $200, a 100×-wrong money figure the user never stated, shown as their own goal and durably persisted on Save | the integer regex `\d{1,3}(?:,\d{3})*|\d+` tries the comma-grouped branch first; for "20000" `\d{1,3}` matched "200", `(?:,\d{3})*` matched ZERO groups, and the trailing tokens are all optional, so the overall match SUCCEEDED without ever backtracking to the `\d+` branch (only the `$` rule lacked a required trailing anchor that would force the retry) | cardinal "the AI never originates a number; financial math is sacred" — a silently-corrupted, never-stated money figure presented as the user's own | `tests/unit/assistant-savings-goal-by-date.test.ts` (`hostile-critic #126 regression locks` → "$20000"=2000000, "$5000"=500000, "$1234"=123400, plus the grouped/suffix/≤3-digit forms still correct); fix = require ≥1 comma-group (`\d{1,3}(?:,\d{3})+|\d+`) so ungrouped numbers fall through to `\d+`. Found by the hostile critic before ship (wf_3de855be). |
| 2026-06-28 | LIVE SimpleFIN ingest: an OVERDRAWN deposit account was stored as a POSITIVE asset and a positive-principal loan / keyword-less liability as a CHECKING asset — both INVERT that account's net-worth sign on the owner's real connected accounts | `mapSimplefinAccount` did `Math.abs(balance)` on EVERY account (discarding the overdraft sign) and `inferAccountType` defaulted keyword-less names to CHECKING, rescued to a liability only by a NEGATIVE balance — so a loan reporting positive principal escaped the rescue | "financial math is sacred — a balance's net-worth sign must be correct for every account state, on every provider" | `tests/unit/simplefin-map.test.ts` (sign-correctness: overdraft stores −, owed card +|owed|, positive-principal servicer loan typed LOAN with +|owed|, each hand-verified through `netWorthCents`) + the broadened `inferAccountType`. Fix: signed balance for assets, `\|owed\|` for liabilities (SimpleFIN gives no liability sign convention); liability-keyword branch before the generic "credit" rule. Found by the live-ingest contract audit (wf_6eade83c, DECISIONS #127). |
